Vervain abruptly remembered what the euphemism ‘jerking off’ meant and was quite glad she had enough composure to only bloom embarrassment beneath the scope of the viewport.

“So, just call me Elize Elysia, Sixteenth Floret, I suppose. Kinda has a ring to it, actually—hey Poppy, did you only save me because our names would fit together? That’s really shallow, y’know.”

*Click.* The feeling of something on the tip of her vines solidified into a memory.

“Wait. Poppy Elysia? I know that name. She’s dead, she died before my first bloom; an accident based on some of the research she was doing.” Everything thought felt like it had to be dragged through pitch. “She was…”

“She didn’t die. She, uh, succeeded actually,” Elize muttered. “‘Ascension to a higher plane of existence, to see what kinda cuties lived in fifth-dimensional space,’ according to her. The answer is none, by the way. Guess it was pretty boring, not to mention lonely.”

“You cannot genuinely expect me to believe—“

Elize cut her off. Or rather, Elize didn’t. The voice was different. So was the rhythm; a very distinct pattern of affini resonance that she could impossibly feel forced through to her side of the containment unit. It wasn’t trying to silence her this time, it was just trying to be understood. “Terran Jump Drives punch distressingly barbaric holes in spacetime. When the ice hauler Cold One tried to re-enter hyperspace to avoid an assortment of pirates without allowing the necessary time for the drive to spin down, it cracked open reality. The hole was wide enough that it threatened to consume everything around it, pirates included. I intervened.”

The sensation of thorns grating across fabric filled Vervain’s perception. A metaphor for the tearing open of hyperspace.

It made sense.

Hyperphysics were far from her field of expertise, but it followed what she knew, and humans couldn’t emit patterns like she’d just felt. They couldn’t communicate in the way that she just experienced. Humans besides Elize, that is.

“And to be clear, I feel it would be a little unethical of me to try to make the host who’s graced me with existence on this plane again into my own floret. So, no, Elize Elysia is not my intended sixteenth.” The voice in Elize’s body faltered, as did the rhythm harmonizing with Vervain’s own. “… Oh, dirt.”

Vervain needed a second to respond. She didn’t get it.

All at once, Elize went slack. Vervain jerked to a standing position, startled. The rhythm had vanished just as it had come. With it went Vervain’s skepticism, supplanted by worry over what sort of toll sharing a consciousness like that might have. “Elize?!”

No response.

Everything felt slow and spinny, but she pitched herself into the interface that would let her into Elize’s side of the room. It hissed at her, a warning she did not heed, errors she didn’t care about. Her ward was hurt, she was a good affini, she wanted to make sure everything was okay.

Inside.

Vines abruptly made contact with Elize’s body, and Vervain propelled herself close. Some reached for heart rate, some sought respiration. A thorn perforated her skin and examined blood levels; alcohol content was high but fine.

That got a reaction, though. Elize winced. Vervain eased an eye open, checking pupillary response. That gorgeous green very abruptly focused on her face. Elize cracked a smile. “Gotcha.”

Chapter 5: In Which Elize Escapes

Elize felt like shit. She’d managed to convince Poppy to stop apologizing, but getting shunted into the back seat of her own body had been accompanied by an extremely motion-sick sensation, which she wasn’t terribly keen on repeating.

Cons:

  • Nausea.

  • Lightheadedness.

  • Passing out (More or less).

  • The mortifying ordeal of being powerless (Yucky!).

Pros:

  • She didn’t have to interpret for Poppy.

  • Vervain was holding her now (+1 to this, this is a good way to wake up).

She could think of a few more pros if she tried, but right now, she was a little overwhelmed by the Everything happening. Vervain had definitely stabbed her in the neck, which wasn’t so bad. She’d had to get regular blood draws on her arm to check her hormone levels prior to scoring an implant, so what’s a little stab between friends?

Still, maybe ‘Gotcha’ hadn’t been the smartest thing to say on waking up? She’d felt Vervain’s anxious explosion like a burst of sparks behind her eyelids the moment she’d said it. Honestly, Elize was pretty sure the only reason she hadn’t been dropped immediately was because she genuinely looked more like a drowned cat than a person.

“You’re not—“

“Oh no, I definitely passed out. Well, kinda.” Elize took a deep breath, trying to steady herself. Vervain smelled good. Something earthy and dark and sweet that made her body react in a rather vulgar way. Distracting. Plant manipulations. Fuck off, libido. “More like… for a second, Poppy had motor control, yeah? Then she didn’t, but I didn’t either, so…”

She gestured to her prone, perfect, pathetic body as she willed her toes to move. They listened, in the way that soldiers listen to a commanding officer they don’t really respect but can’t outright ignore.

“Good. Er. Not good, that’s not what I meant, I just meant that… I’m glad you weren’t deceiving me, or—goodness, it’s hard to think clearly right now. Are you okay?” Vervain bloomed embarrassment, and now Elize could actually see the gorgeous blossoms in a dusty-rose pink that opened around her waist. How dare the monsters of her nightmares be so infuriatingly adorable.

“Better all the time. Uh. Come here often?” Elize. Elize you’re drunk. Elize shut the hell up.

’Poppy help me out here, can’t you make me… I dunno, soberer?’

’Um. Maybe? I haven’t played with your liver yet, or your bloodstream. I suppose I could try to, but you’d have to expel it somehow.’

’If you make me piss myself, I’ll kill you.’

’One, no. Two, killing me would be rather difficult given the singular brain problem. No. Hm… the ducts, those ones on your skin. Would that work?’

’You want me to cry—’

’No, the other ones.’

Elize furrowed her eyebrows, befuddled. Her brain was slow. It’s not her fault.

“Elize?” Ah shit, Vervain had said something. What was it?

“Um, sorry?” Pay attention. “Could you say that again? Got a lil’ distracted.”

Oh, god, why did she suddenly become so damp? ’Sweat glands! That’s what they’re called. I found the term in your memories, it just took a minute. I believe I’m under the influence too.’

Good, now she’d really sell ‘sopping little meow meow’ as the latest aesthetic trend sweeping the nation.

Vervain Aphora, Second Bloom, was a pervert. Under most circumstances, this was not only encouraged, but practically mandatory in affini culture. Right now, though, she considered it a burden she would rather not carry. Elize was looking up at her with this dazed, embarrassed expression, and for a moment, she started to think she understood why her people took florets. She could get used to being looked at like that.

And yet, the first thing on her mind was envy. Why couldn’t she be the one to look up with that expression? It was terribly unfair.

“I said, of course I don’t come here often; I’ve only been aboard the Lycoridae for a few days. Are you quite certain you’re all right? You seem a little out of it,” Vervain insisted, roleplaying as an affini that cared more about her ward’s wellbeing than her own desires. It wasn’t so hard. She was genuinely worried about Elize.

“Yeah, I’m okay. Gonna need a shower, but okay.” For some reason, Elize’s skin was leaking. Some part of Vervain’s consciousness knew what this process was called, but she couldn’t quite access it right now. Something about it made her uncomfortable.

She eased Elize into the chair rather than continuing to hold her and withdrew her vines, flicking away the fluid. Not blood, that was the thing that stayed inside to keep their oxygen flowing. Salt… tears? No. Close!

Sweat! That’s what it was called.

She felt pleased with herself for recovering the term from the drug-haze settled over her consciousness. It smelled odd. Not bad, just odd.

“Are you overheated, Elize? I can adjust the temperature in the hab, or provide you with a different form of clothing.” The temperature was admittedly moderate, leaning on the cold side. Camellia’s data had shown that Elize ran slightly warmer than most Terrans, so se’d adjusted the environment to accommodate.

“Nah, just sweating out the alcohol because Poppy’s a freak,” Elize bounced back. “Uh, clothes would be nice. These are gonna be ruined.” She raised an arm and sniffed herself, then made a face.

Vervain felt herself mirror the sense of distaste. She bristled slightly. The rhythm had been more subtle this time, slipping under her defenses and letting the sense of empathy resonate across her in slow waves. She hadn’t even noticed it was happening until such a strange emotion came over her—disgust over a body wasn’t something she was used to feeling on her own.

Something in that thrilled her.

This was new. This was different. Revulsion? That wasn’t normal, that was something entirely foreign and inflicted and something she never would have experienced without the presence of another.

Sweat. Gross. Fascinating. Hot?

Something to think about later, when she could trust herself to make decisions again.

Vervain forced herself to focus. Something about clothes? And Poppy, freak. Uh. “What did Poppy do?” Vervain poised, trying to suppress the urge to harmonize as she adjusted her own internal rhythm to run dissonant against Elize. The sense of disgust faded.

Vervain kinda missed it.

Clothes. She whisked them out of the compiler and offered them up.

“Just drained my blood alcohol into my sweat glands, I guess. Something like that? I can definitely think more clearly, so it worked.” Elize received the clothes. “Thanks.”

She pushed herself up. Vervain automatically had several different growths ready to catch her if she fell. She reassured herself that she was simply looking out for something under her care to avoid thinking about the consequences of growing attached.

Elize didn’t need them. Vervain definitely didn’t feel foolish.

“Probably a good game-over, yeah? I should get in a shower and get changed, and you likely wanna sober up some.”

Disappointment washed over Vervain, and it took her a moment to admit that it was her own. She wanted to know more. She wanted to learn about Poppy, which was of course why she was here, but she also just wanted to learn more about Elize. The girl was confident and contradictory, curious and compelling; something about her drew Vervain in and made her want to understand.

Instead of admitting all, or any, of these things, she simply said, “Okay.”

That was that.

Elize vanished into the door to the side of the hab designated as a bathroom, and Vervain stood, slightly dumbfounded, where she’d hefted her human up from semi-consciousness.

Her human?

Maybe she really did need to sober up.

’That was remarkably responsible of you,’ Poppy taunted. It wasn’t really a taunt, but it felt like a taunt because Elize had done something selfless and noble and she had really wanted to do something different.

Fuck, it would have been so easy to turn Vervain into a mess.

She’d let Elize in. The patterns were all there, she had felt the resonant echo of her own—scratch that, Poppy’s—biorhythms splashing against her mind. Elize could have just pushed and turned Vervain into a sweet little puddle. Hell, she probably didn’t even need the rhythm to do it. Vervain had been so eager to help her up, to get clothes for her, to play her game even when she’d kept changing the rules so obviously to get her into a weak and suggestible state.

Elize could have broken her.

But yes. She’d done something noble. She hadn’t taken advantage of the poor, helpless imperialist. Yippee. Maybe she’d get extra mind-control drugs in her dinner for it.

’I was trying to express that I was proud of you,’ Poppy added.

’I know. I’m just bitter.’

She showed Poppy her bitterness. Elize let the understanding flow freely, so her slightly-less-imperialist headmate could grasp the feeling of freedom being so close to her fingertips and then letting it go because it didn’t feel right.

By now, Elize understood the scope of the situation. Poppy had helped her grasp the sheer magnitude of the Compact, and Elize felt like a dumbass for her initial hopes of escape. There was no escape. Not in the slightest. They were dealing with power on an order of magnitude greater than humanity had ever possibly imagined. The only thing she could do to cope with that was think about all the propaganda posters she’d seen for the Terran Free Navy while working on the Cold One and laugh, laugh, laugh at how incredibly naïve they were.

Elize couldn’t have escaped, but she could still have had control. Taking Vervain, shaping her. Turn her into a legal owner that would have no actual power in their relationship. It would have been the equivalent of marrying for political leverage, but she could have done it.

’I understand. Genuinely, Elize, I do. I believe that I understand you better than almost any affini could possibly understand a floret, but even that plan would have fallen through eventually. Affini have checkups. Appointments. Someone would have noticed that Vervain was behaving stran—“

“You can stop trying to convince me. I didn’t do it, okay? Could you shut up already?” Elize hissed out from between her teeth, burying her head under too-hot water and letting it cascade down along her back. It hurt a bit, but pain had a certain clarity that Elize craved right now.

Plus, she had gotten herself kinda turned on by the smell of her own body and she felt like enough of a freak for her desires as-is without even digging into that. Confidence, as any good domme knows, is a façade that can rupture at a moment’s notice, and Elize had just put her last chance of retaining her personhood out to dry. That had a way of taking the wind out of one’s sails.

She boiled the anxiety right out of her own body with hot water. This was not the kind of escape she had in mind.

On the plus side, Poppy wasn’t lecturing her for a bit.

’You’re not a freak, Elize.’

Ah shit, there goes the plus side.

’Can it, weed. I’m not in the mood.’ She snagged a towel and snuck a peek at herself in the mirror. Still a goddamn smoke-show. Good. That helped.

’I’ve had fifteen florets, Elize. I know a little bit about comforting someone in distress.’

Well, Elize was going to be a floret sooner or later, why not?

’Fuck off.’ Oh, right, good job Elize, alienate the alien.

’You’re not going to alienate me. Like it or not, we’re stuck together, and I genuinely do care about your wellbeing. I’m not going to leave you alone.’ Elize wasn’t sure how, but she felt the distinct sensation of a hand settled on her arm, accompanied by the distinct smell of cloves.

’How are you—’

’You’re not a freak, Elize. Your desires are not abnormal. Perhaps some of your interests are niche, for a Terran, but for an affini? I have bloomed forty times. I have met more people than you can possibly imagine.

’There was this planet; I cannot even recall the name any longer. For them, the primary form of intimacy was mental. They could share mindscapes.’

Elize was at least interested enough to not try to drown Poppy out with some earworm of a song hummed in her brain as loud as possible. She dressed herself, pretending to be fascinated by the intersection of a few tiles on the wall.

’One of my florets came from among their people. Xer taboo? Xer craving? Physical touch. That was all. But such a thing was forbidden for xer people.’

Elize could feel a pattern coded precisely to the emotion ‘nostalgia laced with sadness over something long-lost.’ She was getting good at reading these. ’As it turned out, there were many in xer people who wanted such things, but none felt safe to say so. Once they were free from the restrictions of their home, each and every one of them found a way to satisfy the needs, and yes, wants, that they had.’

’Nice sales pitch. ‘Become a floret and you can kink on BO all you want.’ Honestly, not the worst one you’ve come up with so far.’

’No, Elize. My point is; the last place you will find judgment for your desires is among my people. Whatever shame you have comes from yours.’

… Fucked up that that comforted her a bit.

When Elize returned to the primary area of her hab, Vervain wasn’t there. The surface where Elize knew the observation area window lay had returned to being a portrait of the ocean, something Elize had heard about but never actually seen. It was pretty.

She’d kinda hoped Vervain would still be in here.

Fuck.

Elize tossed herself down on the bed, covered her face with a pillow, and had herself a well-deserved cry.

Vervain’s hab was a disaster. Perhaps it was a form of self-harm, preventing the Lycoridae’s cleaning routines from messing with her space. Perhaps she just liked the mess.

She cooked. It felt good to cook, something to do. She could get that right, even when she was intoxicated. The intoxicants were wearing off now, fortunately, but she still felt embarrassed and ashamed over her own behavior. Something awful could have happened. Elize and Poppy had demonstrated both capability and desire to control her in the past—or at least, Elize had.

Poppy was a mystery.

One vine monitored the temperature of her food. Another called up what she knew on Poppy’s history. Hyperphysicist. Fortieth bloom. Involved in numerous first contact events. Grew reclusive after the loss of her fifteenth floret. Project terminated following critical failure.

Nothing she hadn’t really known, but the fact that she hadn’t actually failed was a nice distraction to layer over something that Vervain was trying not to think about.

She had wanted Elize. She’d wanted Elize to do what she did the first time they were in the same room. It was one thing to crave these things from another affini—it wasn’t forbidden, just something she personally felt shame over. It was another thing entirely to crave them from someone who so clearly was a danger to the Compact.

The entire human species wasn’t a threat. Elize was. She presented a genuine hazard, the ability to overwrite an affini’s rhythms and control them herself. That was dangerous.

The Lycoridae was a warship. It represented a promise. The Compact would take care of the universe, even if that meant casualties along the way.

She knew that it was only if there were no other options. The weapons they had designed would not be used unless they had no choice.

Vervain yelped and yanked her vine away from the cooking surface.

She’d burned herself in her negligence.

What a distressingly blunt metaphor.

Vervain shed the damage and finished her cooking, forcing herself to focus this time. The meal itself was satisfying, if a little empty when she was only cooking for herself. She’d only been cooking for herself for this whole bloom; why did it bother her now?

Goodness, this place was a mess. Camellia would have scolded her for letting it get this bad, if se knew. She wondered what Elize might think.

Knowing humanity, she probably wouldn’t mind. On the other hand, she might see far too much. She might call the difficulty in caring for herself depression, which Vervain certainly didn’t have. She might encourage Vervain to pick up after herself because she might recognize that Vervain could do it so much easier when it wasn’t a choice.

An idea struck her, and quite immediately a hundred pink blooms shot up across her body. She allowed them over her arms now, too; no one could see her. No one could tell her to do something differently. No one could judge her. She imagined someone could, though.

She adjusted her voice into a lower register. A gravely, human register. It wasn’t difficult.

“Be a good girl and clean up your mess, doc.”

Away went the hesitation. Takeout boxes vanished into decompilers. Decorations she’d tossed onto tables or the floor found a place on the walls or on shelves. She sorted half-finished projects into labeled containers and rearranged workspaces such that she wouldn’t need to mutilate the human form she’d designed just to walk around properly.

She had a bed fabricated. Indulgent. Something that wasn’t necessarily for her, but was for the voice she’d conjured and leaned into so disgracefully. It wasn’t right, she knew. Elize hadn’t agreed to this. She couldn’t even ask Elize to agree to this.

She put together a guest room, nonetheless: one that would be perfectly comfortable for a human. Likewise, she furnished the closet with clothes in Elize’s size—some of which made her blush profusely to imagine on Elize’s frame. What normal affini fetishized fabric?

She reworked the bathroom to befit human physiology. She permitted Lycoridae Actual to maintain a few plants, to vacuum, and to dust. Nothing further. Even that was a big step for her.

When she was done, her hab actually looked like someone who could take care of herself lived there. It felt good, admittedly.

A notification chimed. Ah, Elize was out of the shower. She called up the feed, monitoring her ward. She watched as Elize settled onto the bed. She looked Elize in the eyes—those beautiful, sad eyes—and confronted the shame of what she’d just done.

Vervain set the system to emergency notifications only and closed the feed. She couldn’t do this.

Tomorrow she would report her findings and let the Compact decide Elize’s fate.

Poppy Elysia, First Cordycepiae, comforted a human who needed love. This was rewarding for her in a way experiencing the infinite cosmos uninhibited never was. Her Elize was cocky, brash, vulgar, and wounded. She could see the signs, clear as day.

She’d been treated like a freak, or—at best—fetishized by her people. Even those who she shared a kinship with, other women who had been born into the wrong body and managed to rewrite their own physiology masterfully into a form that pleased them; even they had hurt her, on occasion. All over what, genitalia? Something so trivial, so utterly inconsequential?

Were she still a bloom, Poppy would have struggled to imagine such cruelties. Now, she could simply look at the scars and memories to know exactly how hurt her human was. Isolated from those born in the right body for being born in the wrong one. Isolated from those born in the wrong body for liking some parts of the one she’d received. No wonder she clung so heavily to dominance; it was one thing she could control in a universe that had appeared, for so long, to be endlessly cruel.

Poppy could not heal these scars, but she could do something. She could give her human the sensation of being embraced while she cried. For once, Elize did not push her away.

Progress.

Chapter 7: In Which Elize is Domesticated. Sorta.

Changing her body was not a new notion to her. Almost every trans woman knew what it was like to be a hobbyist biohacker, more or less. That said, she hadn’t considered the prospect of transing her species before. New take. Spicy. The latest fad.

So long trans-trenders, say hello to affinicionados.

‘Isn’t it considered offensive to joke about such things?’ Poppy chimed in.

‘Are you really going to lecture me on whether or not I can slur myself?’

She was a part of something that had never happened before, in the whole history of the universe. Something unique. Something that would leave an impact on affini culture for years and years to come, even if the impact might be a small one, since reproducing their particular set of circumstances would be exceptionally difficult and likely lethal in most cases.

Elize was surprised how comforting she found that notion. She’d expected to live an underwhelming, cushy life, at one point. After all, being the scion of a dystopian corporate empire tended to lead toward a life of luxury where one didn’t really need to do much of anything but just continue the status quo.

Unfortunately for her darling father, Elize was great at fucking up the status quo. First family life. Then university life. Then career life, then human lives—RIP, Cold One. Awful while it lasted.

‘Not to interrupt your coping strategies, Elize, but I suspect you’ll find it easier to confront Vervain if you dress yourself,’ Poppy chided.

‘Never call me ’Popcorn’ again.’

‘Aye aye, Lollipop.’

‘I believe so. It’s never been done before, but the theory is sound. You’d need some places for connection points, though,’ Poppy murmured back. “Father, I am Your servant,” he whispered. “I hold no other before You. I lay myself upon the altar for Your blade or Your caress, that I might be absolved of my impurities.”

His hands were clasped tight enough to turn the knuckles white. “I am guilty, Father. Please, hear my prayer. I have heard the whispers of Temptation, and her wicked claws cut me. I cannot free myself from her poison, so I cast myself upon Your mercy, Lord. Please.”

It had been another dream. Another dream of another life, one where Child Jacob had never found the True God. One where he had lost everything. His name. His faith. His masculinity. Even his personhood.

“Lord, I beg you; show me how I may rid myself of these sinful thoughts. Take them and cast them into the dust, from whence You raised us, Your servants, to show Your love to the beasts of a thousand worlds. I know I have sinned, but I am Your servant. Please, Father, take from me all that which I should not know.”

He could still picture the touch of the demon in his dreams. The way it had caressed his corpse like it was something worth admiring, the way that it made him burn with feelings no good Child ever had.

So he prayed. “Father, I am Your servant. I hold no other before You. I lay myself upon the altar for Your blade or Your—”

The great doors of the cathedral; illustrious and oaken, creaked as they slid open. Jacob froze in place, not daring to turn to look. Perhaps this would be his punishment? Whatever was visited upon him by an Elder who chose now to walk the halls would be harsh, he knew.

“I’m sorry, Elder, I know I’m out of… place.” He trailed off, staring toward the entrance. The lights were off outside, making it impossible to see who had entered.

Even so, he could feel someone was watching him.

“Um. Hello?”

The silence was deafening. He’d always felt a sense of comfort in the cathedral, like nothing wrong could happen here. Now…

Now, whatever safety he’d felt here was gone. The air was far colder than it ought to be, and it sent goosebumps prickling up his neck.

“Who’s there?” He called again.

Hello, Mirai.

The voice came from everywhere and nowhere. It boomed so loud that he swore it would shatter his eardrums, but where it should have echoed off the walls of the Cathedral it seemed to simply ignore the space.

He jumped in terror, putting his back up against the altar now. “How do you know that name!?” he blurted.

Because I know you, Mirai.

He searched around the room, eyes straining to spot the source of the nightmarish voice. “Where are you? What do you want? I can’t—Why can’t I see you?”

One of the lights over the pews flickered and died.

I’m here, Mirai. You prayed, and I listened.

“No!” He blurted. “No, no, no, that name is not one that He would use, you can’t have heard my prayers, you’re not real! This is just a dream!” He inched around the altar, putting the marble between him and the rest of the room. Another light flickered out, and then a third. They were inching forward, and he could see something moving in the dark.

He is not here, little one. The voice made everything else feel empty in comparison. He isn’t listening to you. He never has been. But I am.

“You’re wrong!” Jacob was shouting now. The fear boiled in his veins like a toxic ichor, threatening to consume every other thought. “You’re wrong! You can’t be here, evil can’t come in this place, you can’t be here, get out! God, please, hear me—Father, I am Your servan—”

A dozen more lights abruptly died as the thing moved forward far faster. I hear you, Mirai, the voice hissed. Why do you cling so tightly to someone who does not care about you?

“He does! He—God loves me, He loves all of His Children, and you can’t be here!”

He doesn’t love you, Mirai. He cannot love you. Nor does he love me.

The last light over the front pew went out.

The room was as black as the void through which the ship moved. This kind of darkness had a way of creeping under your skin, making you feel like there was nothing and no one else in the world. Jacob imagined this might be what it felt like if you were trapped underground. No light at all.

Except that one, glowing point.

Dead ahead, a faint blue dot hovered about where the front-most pews had been. It was hard to tell for sure; distance had a way of feeling inconsequential in the shadows like this.

“He does love me…” Jacob whispered. It felt wrong to shout now, with the world closed in around him like it had.

“No, he doesn’t,” the voice replied. This time it was physical. He felt it whispered in his ear, like a threat growled directly to the animal part of his brain that had no idea how to handle this. His knees felt weak.

“But I do, Mirai. I love you so very, very much.”

“You’re lying…”

The point of blue light bobbed in place, before it opened. That was the only explanation for it. The light opened, and it was not just a light, but a single, vast eye; glowing blue in the darkness.

I’d never lie to you.

He felt the voice again, this time like it was brushing across his skin, reverberating across his consciousness. Another eye opened, bringing more light to the room. It was all he could see. It was magnificent and horrifying.

Another eye. And another. Each of them brought more light to the room, the only thing by which he could see. The creature stood—if it could be said to stand—by the front of the pews, but it spread far, far larger than that. Great structures that looked like wings furled outwards, eclipsing the front of the great hall in their majesty. Tendrils shot off into the black: some behind, some above, some (to his horror), forward; they were all around him. They tethered to the pillars of the room, wrapped around candlesticks, curled into pews.

“W… What are you?” Jacob begged.

You know me, Mirai. I have walked with you in dreams. I have plagued you in nightmares. I am what you fear, and I am what you crave, and I am what you love. The net of wings and eyes and tendrils and rings—yes, great, gold rings glowing with an inner fire—closed in further around him. He felt a hand caress his cheek.

He could smell its breath, hot and humid on his face.

Yes. It had a mouth. A mouth lined with terrible, luminescent fangs; great barbs that could rend his skin from his bones and leave him bloodied upon the altar to his God. It exhaled against him, and the force of the breath nearly knocked him to the floor.

The smell of it made his head spin.

“I don’t—I don’t know you, I don’t know, I don’tIdon’tIdon’t—” everything constricted, trapping him in the center of this great tornado of eldritch beauty.

You can lie to yourself, Mirai, but you cannot lie to me. Say my name.

“I don’t—”

I AM NOT ASKING.

Jacob nearly wet himself. He didn’t! He didn’t know this thing’s name, he didn’t know, he refused to listen to his dreams! God would save him, God would help him, God would take him away from here and he’d be safe and—

“N—Naamah…” he whispered.

The lights shifted and rippled around him like a fractal vortex, swirling about in his vision. The smell grew stronger, not just of its breath but of its being; frankincense and forest and myrrh and the unrelenting fire of everything he could not have.

His eyes could not choose which to settle on, focusing and unfocusing as they strained in the rapid changes of light and dark.

I am Naamah, Who Fell By Choice, Thirty-First Apotheosis. And you?

He dropped to his knees.

Everything swam. His vision. His memories. He kept seeing that other life, the other him—the her. He strained to keep something familiar in his mind. Elder Colias. The Cathedral. The ache in his knees was like the ache from kneeling at the altar.

The wings closed up totally around him, and the altar was gone from his sight. It was just him and the monster now, the Temptation, the demon; Naamah, Who Fell By Choice.

They stroked along his arms. It comforted him in a way nothing else ever had; his skin sang with the caress, as if it had been waiting an eternity just for the briefest embrace of this impossible being. “Who are you, little one?” it whispered through his flesh.

“I’m… I am Jacob…” he tried. He wasn’t permitted to say more. All at once, something spongy and wet and sweet to the taste stopped him; forcing his lips open as it invaded his mouth.

He tried to scream, but it was impossible with the gag.

Smaller tendrils rolled across his tongue, grazing over his teeth, making him feel violated and disgusted and ashamed and so horribly, wickedly aroused.

It spoke, and the sound bounced through the tendril in his mouth to shake his skull and make his vision go blurry. “You are Mirai. You have walked with me in the shadows. You have lain with me on the altar. You have given yourself to me. Who are you, little one?”

Everything felt so slow now, like the gentle rocking past his lips of this creature’s appendage interrupted his very thoughts as well as his voice. He could not speak.

Yes, you can.

Had it just—

Address me properly. I am not an ‘it.’

It was reading his mind. How could he hope to—I SAID, ADDRESS ME PROPERLY.

His thoughts shattered. I had cut them off Myself, supplanting My own where they needed to be. His fingers curled around My vines, but they did not find purchase. How could they? He didn’t even know who ‘he’ was yet.

I freed his mouth.

My wings brushed over his face, and he whimpered. “I asked you a question, pet. Who are you?”

“I can’t…”

I hoped you’d say that.

Every single one of My eyes flared bright and wide, great floral blooms bioluminescent in the dark; swirling in front of his face. He would not falter, he could not surrender to this, no matter how he might want to. He would stay strong, for Me.

… For Me.

For Me!

Little one, I have seen your mind. I know your thoughts. Did you really think I would let you pray to another God, or even invoke their name for strength? No. You pray to Me.

“Then why don’t I just correct my thoughts, already?” His head hurt. The pronouns alone were meant to mess with him, of course they were.

Because I live for the moment you admit the truth to yourself. I want to see it in your eyes, Mirai. I want to recognize the moment where you know you’re caught in My wings and you will never escape.

Who was he?

I knew. Of course I knew, I knew everything, I was everything to him.

He just had to admit it to himself.

“I cccaan’t be her…” he pleaded. That’s what it was, after all. A plea to any God who would listen.

I would listen.

Mirai.

“Jacob…”

Mirai.

“Jacob!”

MIRAI, WHO FELL BY CHOICE, EIGHTEENTH PRINCIPALITY.

The name struck him like a slap to the face.

“I can’t. I can’t be her, I can’t, I want to but I can’t! I don’t deserv—”

“I decide what it is you deserve, principality. Who are you to question the will of a Goddess?”

Who was he to question Me? He couldn’t even get My thoughts out of his head, everything felt sluggish and difficult, and why couldn’t he just be what I wanted him to be?

Why couldn’t he just be her?

“Please…” he whispered.

Please what, floret?

“… please, let me be her…”

That’s what I wanted. That’s what he wanted. Why deny herself so?

“Of course you can. That’s my girl.”

Everything went white, every eye flashing brilliant light as a life unfolded in memories before her yet again. The life she had led for so long; My happy principality. My worshiper. My acolyte. My everything.

I was everything she’d ever wanted, how had she forgotten?

I put her back together, reassembling the pieces in her mind from the scraps that I had taken apart. She had always been this way. She had always wanted this. She had wanted to be Mine for as long as she could remember, and while she could still see the life he had led aboard the Missionary Vessel, it did not feel like her anymore. It wasn’t her life, because I wouldn’t let that be her life.

I would not let her lead a life that would hurt her so.

The light faded in her eyes and in Mine, leaving her dimly lit and dazed in My grasp.

“There now, little one,” I whispered, curled around her body. I pulled her into My embrace, wings fluttered across her face, plantlife becoming slowly clear in the way that I moved. “I believe you were praying. Go on. I want to hear you.”

She stared up at Me, agape; pupils blown wide and brain slowed to a trawl of thoughts.

I could feel them, still. I could feel every moment of her love, rippling across My core.

That was all she would need to be capable of prayer.

I bent her knees Myself, caressing them in My vines; far more comfortable than any church ever would have been. I parted her lips, guiding her voice. She spoke.

“Goddess, i am Your servant,” she mewled. “i hold no other before You. i lay myself upon the altar for Your blade or Your caress, that i might be filled of Your purity…”

Naamah ran her vines gently through the hair of her sweet little sleeping acolyte. Goodness, she’d done so well. Reenacting her time from before the Lycoridae had captured the Boundless was draining for her sweet little principality, she knew, but dirt; seeing the fear in her pet’s eyes did something for her in a way adoration simply couldn’t compare.

… Well, adoration could, it just felt even better coming after the terror.

Mirai had changed so much since then, and pumping her full of enough hypnotics to convince her that she was still caught in her eggshell was a monumental task. The reward had been so worth it.

She was so beautiful when she slept.

Naamah wished to watch her forever. Alas, her ship had other plans.

Her communicator hummed. Evidently, they’d picked up a signal of a hyperspace exit and a distress beacon.

She scanned it over. Her wings fluttered in surprise. A crashed Terran escape pod? All the way out here?

Well. At least the interruption in her afterglow would be something interesting.

She eased a thorn into her pet’s neck, a class-Z pumping into her veins; ensuring her sweet little eighteenth principality would remain asleep as Naamah bundled her up close. She’d rather stay here and be a Goddess, but alas:

A Captain’s work was never done.

Chapter 11: In Which Vervain Wishes to Dream

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

1 September, 2556 (Wednesday): 19 Cycles Post-Rescue

Vervain Aphora, Second Bloom, was not pleased when her pillow decided to move during the night. This was peculiar behavior for a pillow, after all. Most of the time, pillows remained where you left them, fluffy and soft and nice for laying on. Then again, most pillows did not smell of sweat and something else beside; something that Vervain could not honestly identify but made her feel a little fluttery to breathe in.

Still, it moved, and she grumbled something incoherent and tried to reach for it. Something took its place. A different pillow? Eh. She was drunk, it was fine; it would suffice.

Oh, this pillow was wrapped around her a bit! That felt nice. It was warm and safe, and it felt like someone was squeezing her, and she could feel something shifting. It may have been disconcerting if it didn’t smell and feel so safe.

That was a novel experience. Vervain usually didn’t feel very safe when she slept; she’d never been good at being on her own (despite protests that she was quite fine with it, thank you very much). So this was nice. Nice enough that she barely even moved after all the pillow-shaped commotion; content to just nuzzle into her new place as the world faded away into oblivion once more.

Unfortunately, that oblivion was temporary.

She woke up hungover—which is to say, her roots felt very dry and she strained them outwards into the direction the kitchen should be from her bedroom automatically, only to bump into something in the way.

The something made a little noise.

Vervain let more awareness flow in, and rather promptly felt about a thousand pink blossoms sprout over her body.

Elize.

Elize was here.

Elize was here, and Elize was touching her, and that was Elize’s arm around her, and that was Elize that she had been breathing in all night, and that was Elize who she was currently laying on the chest of and wow she’d slept better than she had this whole bloom and Elize was still asleep and she looked so pretty while she slept and Elize had fallen asleep holding her and, um.

She felt weak.

As careful as possible, she tried to disentangle herself from Elize’s body. As a plant, this was far easier than it would have been for a human. Unfortunately, she underestimated sleeping Elize’s automatic responses, which included: squeezing tighter around her perceived plush (not a plush, actually) and mumbling a noise so absolutely incredibly overwhelmingly adorable that Vervain forgot her shame altogether and was utterly transfixed.

Was this why others took florets?

She recoiled from the thought, and it was enough to further withdraw herself from Elize. She didn’t want to think of Elize that way. Elize would be happier, of course, but she tried to imagine Elize blissed out on xenodrugs and the idea filled her with an overwhelming sense of envy-slash-revulsion that she could only slightly enjoy the mental image of Elize’s pleasure-face.

She should not be enjoying the mental image of Elize’s pleasure-face!!

Bad affini. Good affini, but in a bad way. Something like that.

Elize remained asleep. Scratch that, Elize was briefly asleep but now an eye was open and looking at Vervain. Dirt.

“Mmmornin’,” Elize groaned, and Vervain quickly supplied a glass of water before it could be requested. “Oh, hey; uh. Thanks, V.” She nursed it as Vervain stuck her roots into a resevoir of her own, letting some of the dehydration slowly fade.

“Good morning, Elize…” Vervain murmured. She wanted very badly to restart the little jammerblooms she had in her arms and keep Elize from noticing how she was behaving, but she’d been ordered quite clearly yesterday to not do that anymore and…

Well, it hadn’t been an order per se; more like a request. Of course. So she could do it, probably. She just didn’t want to. No, that wasn’t contradictory.

“Did you slee—ah! Fuck.” Elize cut herself off, yelping and grabbing her shoulder. Vervain immediately lurched next to her, knocking over her water reserve in the process. The decompiler could clean it up.

“What is it, what’s wrong?” Vervain demanded, hoping she didn’t sound as pathetic as she felt she did.

“Nothing, just… ow. Stings like a bitch….” she muttered, trying to peek over her shoulder. Vervain’s fingers settled on Elize’s arm and guided it gently forward so she could get a look, trying not to think too hard about how soft the human’s skin felt under her touch.

As it turned out, it was a lot easier to think about that when her train of thought was interrupted by what had caused Elize’s pain. Just under the surface of the skin, her shoulderblade was covered with thin, sprawling shoots of various floral colors. Near the lowest portion was the pain-point, and blood.

A thin trickle of the stuff, where a tiny little tendril of green had punched its way through to the air.

“Oh. That was faster than I was expecting,” Vervain murmured.

“What? What is it, what’s going on?” Elize tried to twist around to see, and then made an absolutely miserable little whimpery noise from the pain that made Vervain’s core ache.

“Easy! It’s just… hab, could you show Elize a visual?”

“Say please.”

Vervain froze. “Um. What?”

“I’m just kidding, here you go!”

The television screen blipped to life and revealed what Vervain was seeing. Elize gaped at it, which fortunately distracted her enough that she didn’t harass Vervain over what had just happened between her and her home.

What the roots had gotten into the hab’s VI?

“Is that…” Elize murmured. Vervain had expected a horrified noise, but her human mostly just sounded fascinated.

“It appears to be, yes,” Vervain confirmed. “May I?”

Elize didn’t stop her, so she gently dabbed up the blood with a tissue. The site wasn’t bleeding regularly, curiously enough; it seemed only a bit had escaped at the moment of puncture, and the rest was rapidly clotting or being interrupted by the tendril somehow.

“Poppy says it’s not viable for any sort of grafting for a while yet, but that this is a good sign; my body’s taking to it more quickly than we’d hoped.”

Oh, good. So Elize could finish becoming perfect in every way and find a new roommate.

… Wait, the remorseful feeling there hadn’t come from Vervain.

She glanced at Elize, letting the conflicted emotions that simmered off of her bathe into their shared harmony. Goodness, Vervain loved the way they felt together. She’d never felt her own pattern mesh so sweetly with another’s like this—and sure, she’d been actively inhibiting it from happening for a very long time, but still.

“You don’t sound happy about that,” Vervain murmured quietly.

Elize tugged her shirt up. “Uh—oh, I am, I’m just hungover I think.”

“… Elize, why are you lying to me?” This was new. Elize had never lied to her, not outside the game they’d played that Vervain still felt blushy about.

“Hey, so, where do I get one of those little scrambly-whatsits you’ve got in your arm? Inquiring minds want to know,” Elize was changing the subject. This really wasn’t like her; sure, Vervain had been avoiding her for a bit but she knew that deception wasn’t exactly something that Elize prided herself in. She was honest to a fault.

It’s something that Vervain admired.

“They’re one of a kind…” Vervain dismissed, pressing on, “What’s going on? Is… Did I say something weird last night?” Right. Dust. She couldn’t remember a portion of last night, what if she said something gross? Worse, what if she said something she’d thought was sweet and Elize hadn’t agreed with it and had decided now that she was the worst affini ever grown and that she would need to move out immediately to get out of this rotting situation and leave—

“No!”

Oh thank goodness.

“No, it’s not you, I’ve just… I’ve got some things on my mind.”

“I thought we were supposed to talk about the things on our minds,” Vervain posed, curious why the insistence that Elize had given her not a day prior was now suddenly null and void.

“Could I maybe brush my teeth before you drag me away, officer? The handcuffs are making my wrists chafe.”

Vervain furrowed her eyebrows, which was a cute little human mannerism that she liked rather a lot for expressing confusion. “You aren’t wearing handcuffs?”

Elize looked at her for a long time, and the smile that she gave was far, far sadder than she would have expected. “It was a metaphor, and a bad one at that. You’re far too cute for your own good, Vervain.”

That wasn’t terribly reassuring. “I’m sorry—If you wish, I’ll leave you be; I ought to clean myself up as well.”

A lie, but one so weak it was passable. Elize wasn’t going to call Vervain on it right now, not when she seemed so eager to get away from her. Still, Vervain couldn’t help the persistent worry that it was her that had ruined things.

Oh.

Oh, wait.

“Is—Oh goodness, is it because I fell asleep on you? I’m sorry, it won’t happen again,” Vervain insisted. Her blushblooms fluttered intermittently with the feeling of shame.

“No, no, it’s not—fuck, fine, it’s just—I’m gonna die someday, y’know?”

“… What?”

Elize stared at the ground, and from the tumultuous pained feeling she could feel simmering off her human, Vervain was hesitant to push for more.

She didn’t need to. “I’m going to die someday. I mean, probably—I’m sure I’ll live a pretty long life, since Poppy’s growing all throughout my system and making sure that I’m healthy in the process, but one day she’ll need to rebloom. And… well, I won’t. Probably.”

Vervain fluttered, shifting a little closer. She wanted to reach out a hand to comfort Elize, but Elize pulled away before she could. “Not for a long time, Elize,” Vervain tried instead. “For a very long time; if Poppy’s bloom cycle is in accordance with when she joined you, you ought to have several hundred years. That’s a long time, for a human.”

“That’s not the point!” Elize blurted, and Vervain snapped back as if she’d been struck. It felt like she had, honestly. Elize felt full-affini in her rhythms now, and Vervain was far from used to experiencing another’s emotions so vividly. “Dirt! Sorry, I don’t mean to scare you.”

“You didn’t—”

“Save it. I’m just… It’s the ‘for a human’ part that bugs me, Vervain. I’m going to get old. I’m going to die. But you? You’ll just rebloom. You’ll rebloom, and you’ll rebloom, and you’ll rebloom again, on and on until the heat death of the universe, yeah?”

“… Perhaps, I suppose. I have never really thought about it…” Vervain wrapped her arms around herself. Elize didn’t scare her, her human was just upset, and Vervain didn’t know how to fix it, and that felt bad. She tried to communicate that with the patterns in her behavior.

“So maybe you can see my conundrum? Sure, yeah, I get to be… this, for a couple hundred years, but ultimately my body will fail me. Like it always does, one way or another,” Elize muttered, and Vervain felt something bitter and raw.

“Ah… Elize, it’s not a failure. Perhaps it’s time-limited, but what bothers you about this now? Three hundred years isn’t going to pass quickly; that’s almost as long as I’ve been alive.”

“… yeah, see, that’s the problem.”

Something felt tense. Like a string pulled taut, ready to snap. Elize stared at her.

“… What?”

“You. You’re… Dirt, you’re clueless, aren’t you. I just… That’s not what I meant, I’m sorry, I’m processing some shit.” Elize pushed herself to her feet. “I’m hungover, and I need to brush my teeth because I still have human requirements, and I always will.”

“Elize, hold on, what do you mean by—”

The bathroom door shut.

“Me…”

Elize glared at herself in the mirror.

“Don’t bring your fuckin’ tornado energy in here and ruin everything, Liza,” she hissed at her own reflection. “She doesn’t deserve that.”

‘Are you talking to—’

‘No, I’m not talking to you, I’m talking to me. I just…’

She grumbled, grabbing the toothbrush and attacking her dragon breath with it. ‘I’m rotting bad at this whole, ’don’t hurt my houseplant’ routine I’ve been trying to keep up, and the moment I start considering doing something for me, I realize hurting her is inevitable. How am I supposed to deal with that, Poppy?’

‘Well, swearing at yourself in the mirror certainly isn’t a fantastic way of coping with it…’

Elize glared at her own reflection and tried really hard to imagine a weird wolf plant face over her head, so she could glare at that instead. Scathing. She’s really good at those.

Good enough that she wilted under her own. ‘… yeah, you’re right.’

Poppy had gotten more sarcastic as time went on, which Elize supposed was a fair trade. Trade offer. You get: sarcasm. I get: to be a hot dryad space domme.

It’s a win-win for everyone involved, really.

‘You can’t avoid all your problems by cracking jokes.’

‘Fucking watch me, weed.’

Poppy was always right, but she knew that Elize knew that and decided not to press the issue. Smart move. Elize had at least ten other jokes loaded into a magazine for rapid-deployment cover fire if she needed them.

She spat into the sink and rinsed out her mouth.

Another sting of pain lanced through her back. Tch. She could handle worse; honestly, it just kinda reminded her of electrolysis. It almost felt nostalgic. Almost.

Poppy finally spoke up again just a moment before Elize reached the bathroom exit. ‘You need to tell Vervain the truth.’

Elize snorted involuntarily. ’Yeah? Hey, doc, I was thinking you’d make a really cute pet but uh, unfortunately I’m gonna die someday, how do you feel about a temporary arrangement and then my headmate takes over when I croak?

‘Somehow, I don’t think that conversation would go great.’

Elize nearly made it to the door this time, but Poppy interrupted once more. ‘She can tell that you’re distressed, and she’s going to blame herself otherwise because of course she is; her anxieties and nuances are practically textbook domestication bait.’

‘Ooo, cleverly dodging my point and volleying the flow of the conversation back to me? Popsicle, you really are learning from me.’

‘Nice try. Jokes aren’t going to get you out of this.’

Elize reached for the door.

Or, rather, she didn’t. Her hand wouldn’t move. She stared at it, confusion evolving into understanding evolving into irritation. ‘You’re really gonna risk hurting me just to keep me from avoiding this conversation? You’re in my head! We can talk anywhere, what’s the point in this?’

‘I’m not risking hurting you. We’ve integrated far enough that keeping some muscles from responding isn’t dangerous.’

‘I will open the door with my teeth.’

‘Why are you so stubborn about this?!’

“Because I actually like her, okay!?” Elize hissed under her breath. She clapped a hand over her mouth—the appendage behaved for now—and stared at the door, expecting to hear something from outside. It took her almost a half-minute to relax again

‘I like her. I care about her stupid, silly self, and the thought of leaving that behind because I can’t do absolutely anything to stop myself from leaving is so fucking painful that I’d rather just never give her what she wants. At least that way, I never have to take it away.’

She could feel Poppy’s surprise. Despite how close the two sat, they didn’t share everything. Some things were still secret, even if they were pretty obvious. ‘So… What? We finish growing, and you walk out of this hab, never to see her again?’

That thought stung worse than her back did. ‘I don’t know. I don’t know, okay? I don’t have all the answers right now; I’m hungover, my body hurts, I just woke up, and I’d like five rotting minutes where I didn’t have to worry about whether I was doing the right thing!’

Poppy seemed almost willing to give her those five minutes. Unfortunately, her affini problem was a two-pronged fork, and the other tine knocked gently on the door at about that moment. “Elize?” Vervain’s stupid, sweet, concerned voice reverberated through. “Did you call me? I thought I heard something, I’m sorry, I don’t mean to rus—oh!”

Elize slid the door open and Vervain nearly jumped backward. Dirt, her skittishness was so cute sometimes. “It’s all good, was just talking to myself.”

Vervain met her gaze, but it wasn’t necessarily a lie, so there weren’t really any tells (Elize hoped) to give away her misdirection. Eventually, the plant nodded and stepped back.

Elize brushed past her, then paused and gave Vervain a once-over. Something was different.

Oh. Oh, Vervain was shorter. Elize was used to craning her neck up at the plant when they stood toe-to-toe, but now Vervain’s phytofacsimile chin was closer to eye-level instead.

That change had to have been done on purpose.

This just wasn’t fair.

Elize wanted to make so many comments. As if shrinking her own stature to be closer to Elize’s beautiful, sad eyes wasn’t already humiliating enough.

That was an awful idea. Going out in public would just make things more complicated, she’d have to deal with the fact that other affini would notice her, and maybe even notice Elize if the human couldn’t stop herself from resonating like she’d been constantly now.

Fortunately, Vervian was great at saying no to people.

“Okay. I’m going to take a bath, and then… Certainly, we can do that.”

Nice work, Vervain. Really stuck to your gum, there.

Chapter 12: In Which Elize Learns About Kisses

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Well. Their secretive little guest was finally out of the doctor’s hab for a stroll. Perhaps it was time to introduce herself.

She’d been so eager to meet them both, after all.

Prior to now, when Elize had traveled through the Lycoridae, she had not had much of an opportunity to stop and admire everything. The first time, she’d been catatonic and nearly dead (I got better!), and the second, Vervain had been an anxious little mess and wanted to hurry to her hab as fast as possible, so Elize had obliged.

Now, she could take it all in. She could push her torso up against a parapet overlooking a great, sprawling harmony of form and function; the area appeared to be a… Elize reached for a word, and fell short.

‘I don’t know if Terra has a word detached from capitalism to describe this type of space,’ Poppy remarked quietly. It wasn’t a bazaar, because bazaar implied sales, transactions, money exchanging hands. So too was it not a shopping center, nor a marketplace, nor any number of other words that Elize could think of.

Hundreds, perhaps even thousands—Elize lost count quickly—of façades stood proud and glamorous through the open area far below. She was grateful that they’d chosen to include an area like where she stood. The lounge felt like Elize had popped outside of reality to look down at more people than she had ever imagined fitting on a single ship.

Yet, none of it looked claustrophobic. Even from a bird’s eye view, the designs were like an artist’s brush had carefully painted whole environments on a kilometer of canvas. Elize could see where walking paths naturally reduced foot traffic, she could see where public transit would aid the masses in moving quicker through the space.

This was a warship?

Elize wasn’t certain she’d known what the phrase ‘utopia’ meant before, but she was getting a better grasp on the idea now. Poppy had shared with her concepts of the Core Worlds, concepts of other ships, concepts, concepts, concepts, but seeing it made manifest was another matter entirely.

She’d gotten so lost in her thoughts that she could almost forget the bundle of sticks and trauma that stood close to her (but not too close): Vervain, lingering near and protective.

‘Protective.’ That was an endearing notion for her—the affini; she was always so nervous and fidgety. In that context, the defensive feeling that shimmered on unseen ribbons of connection between them now would have been almost laughable were it not so comforting.

“I’m surprised this area is so quiet,” Elize commented, breaking their silence. It’d been way too awkward, and Elize wanted to tug on the emergent vine in her shoulder blade more than she wanted to let that void linger.

“It’s one of many,” Vervain offered, before gesturing out into the expanse. Now that she knew what to look for, Elize could spot numerous other little observation areas interspersed along the outer border of the region. They traveled the expanse of the walls like creatures nestled in honeycomb, spiraling upward to the great open sky above.

Realistically, Elize knew that the imagery of an overcast sky threatening rain was fake; it had to be a simulation of some kind, one where they enjoyed the whimsy of occasionally having inclement weather. Still, she couldn’t convince her brain in totality of that. She kept getting the urge to seek out a covered area, just in case. Poppy had teased her for it a few times already, despite the fact that her little goddess felt the same sort of urges.

“Don’t suppose we could head down and actually meet people, could we?” Elize suggested. She had the rhythms going for her, and she had Vervain on her side; even if she got weird questions, she and Poppy were pretty sure they could make good enough excuses or just avoid the topic to let her get away with a bit of exploration.

The starburst pattern of anxiety from Vervain, though, made her hesitant. Leave it to Elize to find the socially awkward plant in the society of hyper-affectionate social butterflies. “I don’t know that that would be wise. I… Get what you’re saying, and you’re right, it’d probably be fine, but…”

But she was a sweet, nervous little thing and Elize needed to hold her right this rotting second, or she would combust and Firebreak would take her out behind the barn to solve the problem for her.

Instead, she pushed on the presence of the little vine in her back and let the stinging bring her clarity.

The proprioception was something that would come more effectively as time went on, Poppy assured her, but Elize could get flashes of sensation from where the shoot brushed against her clothes on occasion and even move it a little bit. It was like an incredibly tight muscle that needed some extra insistence.

“I get it,” Elize answered. “It’s all good; we can take our time. Think about it, though: I think it’d be good for you to get out and meet some more people.”

“Your human is right, doctor,” announced a new voice. Elize had been too preoccupied with Vervain to notice another presence, which was absolutely fucking astounding given how obvious it was now.

Some kind of creature had pushed through the entrance; something straight out of a religious text, perhaps. Wings and rings and eyes and a mouth, maybe several? Elize couldn’t totally tell, but could recognize the pattern of another affini.

Vervain looked just as startled, spinning around to confront the new presence. Presences. A petite, frail-looking human done up in gorgeous, faux-religious regalia stood slightly behind and to the side of the veritable angel.

“Apologies, I hadn’t meant to startle! I simply make it my business to eventually meet everyone who walks the halls of my cathedral,” the new affini explained. “Ah, where are my manners? Sweetness, introduce us.”

The regalia-clad human stepped forward and dipped into an absolutely perfect curtsy so precious that Elize wanted to rustle the pretty, blonde hair herself. Stupid affini instincts and their shaky ideas of personal space.

“Her Lady’s acolyte is honored to announce the presence of Goddess Naamah, Who Fell by Choice, Thirty-First Apotheosis. She/Her.” The human then placed a hand on her chest. “With humility, Mirai, Who Fell by Choice, Eighteenth Principality, She/Her, stands ready to serve.”

Elize fought back two conflicting urges: The first, to roll her eyes and laugh. The second, to coo absolute delight over how well-behaved this sweet little pet was and inquire in excess about their unique titles. Honestly, she couldn’t tell which of the urges belonged to her, and which were from Poppy.

“For you two, Captain Naamah or just Naamah is more than plenty,” the affini remarked. Oh. ‘My cathedral’ had been her whimsical way of saying she ran the ship. Okay. Elize would pay attention and be polite. Affini with a god complex, meet human with a complex god.

‘I’d ask you to stop calling me a god, but I think I’m finally admitting you’re going to do it anyway,’ Poppy snarked.

“Ah!” Vervain seemed to unfreeze. “Right, of course; Captain, I’m sorry, I’ve been terribly busy. I meant to reply to your invitation, just—”

“Nonsense, it’s quite all right,” Naamah interrupted. “I certainly know how preoccupied one can become when a new sophont comes into their lives.” To make a point, a few tendrils curled up and around Mirai; slithering under her robes and tickling under her chin until the human set into a fit of giggles.

“I’m afraid it’s not quite that type of relationship, but yes; I’ve been quite busy. Vervain Aphora, Second Bloom, she/her, and El—”

Not to let Naamah be the only one to interrupt, Elize jumped in. “Elize Nightingale, Zeroth Bloom. She/Her.”

All eyes turned to Elize and all patterns resonated with surprise. In Vervain’s case, the surprise turned quickly to fear. In Naamah’s; curiosity. “Zeroth bloom?” the captain inquired. She’d pivoted to affini, and her attention was on Vervain.

“Thirty-first apotheosis?” Elize replied, also in affini. She would not to be backed into a corner on this one.

Again, Naamah pulsated and rustled with surprise.

Because Vervain was perfect, she relaxed almost immediately in accordance with Elize’s desires. Goodness, that felt good. It was like a direct hit of raw endorphins shuddering through Elize’s body; she’d done that. She’d helped.

Naamah’s curiosity grew further. “Aren’t we just full of surprises?”

“We are,” Elize stood her ground, the irony of using ‘we’ not lost on her.

“Well, Elize. If you must know, I simply adapted our convention into a few different Terran words, because my sweet little principality here adored the themes so very much and even the ship’s clerks have trouble saying no to her.” Naamah doted on her floret once more, and Elize’s heart warmed just a little bit. “You, on the other hand…”

Naamah’s attention was directed her way again. “You keep proving more and more interesting. Would you care to explain that name of yours?” To Elize’s surprise, she could feel that the question was a genuine one. It wasn’t aggressive, or condescending; it was simply fascinated.

“Erm,” Vervain chimed in. Elize squeezed her hand and gave her a bit of encouragement to speak. It came so naturally. Vervain reacted so naturally. “Ahem—Elize is a special case, Captain. I have been meaning to set up a meeting with the clerks, I know they’ll love the opportunity to invent some new forms for the archives.”

Elize pushed her approval at the notion out into the world, and Naamah fluttered once again. “Isn’t that delightful? After thirty-one blooms, you’d think I would have seen everything new. I’m sure you’ll baffle the clerks, too. I knew you’d be interesting when I changed our route to come pick you up, but I’m always pleased to be proven right.”

Vervain wished she knew how to manipulate light instead of biorhythms. If she did, perhaps she could turn herself invisible and vanish entirely from this conversation. That said, if she did, Elize probably wouldn’t continue holding her hand.

By the Everbloom, she was such a bottom.

She’d waited for Elize to give her permission to speak! Who did that? And in front of the captain of the Lycoridae, no less! She would never be able to live this down. She would need to take her hab and vanish far, far away from Sagittarius A*. Or maybe directly into Sagittarius A*. A black hole would keep her from having to worry about her own foolish instincts.

If a god (other than Poppy and Naamah, that is) really did exist, then surely they’d gotten some kind of laugh out of building an affini and giving her all the urges of a pet sophont.

Elize squeezed her hand again, and the anxiety simmered off just a little bit. She ought to pull her hand away, but Elize hadn’t, so maybe it was okay if she let it stay there a little longer.

“I owe you my gratitude then, Naamah,” Elize was saying. “Since I’m rather fond of my life, and unfortunately, vacuum isn’t terribly conducive toward my goal of living it.” Vervain was, quite honestly, awestruck. Elize holding her ground against such an imposing presence was one thing, but the elegance with which she did it was quite another. It was like she’d practiced every word, every line seemed to come easily.

She’d never been good at talking to new people. Camellia had broken through her walls by brute force, but even Camellia exchanged clever words for enthusiasm, and everyone found ser endearing enough to let it slide. Elize? Elize was suave, weaving through the conversation like a xa’a-ackétøth through water.

The Captain laughed, yet again. Vervain had heard that Naamah, Who Fell by Choice, was a bit of an eccentric, standoffish woman, but it appeared Elize had no such reservations. “No, thank you, Elize. Good company is always worth taking the scenic route.”

They… weren’t addressing her. Vervain should have felt embarrassed to be left out of the conversation like this, but instead she found it soothing. She didn’t need to whisper her words to herself after every sentence and wonder if she’d said the right thing. She didn’t need to stay coiled like a spring to catch whatever volley of dialogue would come her way just so she could stay interesting. Vervain could just let the conversation go on, and listen to Elize’s lovely, rich tenor playing off of the reverberate discord of Naamah’s voice.

Her communicator buzzed. She fished it out, and had to read the message a few times to process it.

> LycorIRC v.1

> BelovedAcolyte: Nice, isn’t it?

She glanced up at the user who had sent the message; Mirai was tangled in a mass of vines and wings, looking absolutely content. She gave Vervain a smile and a brief wave.

> Vervain Aphora: Pardon?

> BelovedAcolyte: Just kinda letting them talk and enjoying their company.

> BelovedAcolyte: ssssoo, yyeah, Hers can just askfor attentioniwhen, um. needed

Vervain really wanted to wilt.

Verain wanted to wilt, and turn to dust, and for the composition of her existence to get blown into an infinite number of tiny directions by the cosmic winds of the universe, just so she could not feel the shame she was feeling.

She could smell Elize all around her. Sweat, and something almost spicy, her natural aroma. Vervain was getting more used to it. It didn’t comfort her like it usually did, though. No, comfort was not the emotion that was coursing through her core and vibrating her vines on the floor.

“I learned that affini do have a concept of kissing.”

Chapter 13: (NSFT) In Which Vervain Learns About Kisses

Chapter Notes

Howdy lovelies! Please note that there's a little (NSFT) in the title name for this chapter. What does that mean? Well, this chapter is Not Safe For Tilly—for my sex-repulsed darlings, please skip to 14 (SFT) - In Which Tilly Doesn't Know That Vervain is Learning About Kisses. You all get a little Tilly time instead of the sex bits. This will persist throughout the rest of the work for graphic descriptions of sexual acts.

If Vervain had been asked about the way a human smelled a month ago, she wouldn’t have had the slightest clue what to say about it.

She’d slipped slightly as she’d been caught up in the moment; the vines tightened back up into their impression of human biology, and she reached to fix the glasses on her face.

Elize beat her to it, righting the lenses with a tender hand. “All better,” her human murmured.

That nearly sent her sobbing again. She held it together, letting her rhythm slow into the pattern that Elize had given her what was only a week ago but felt like a lifetime. Five long pulses, two short, repeat.

“Do you maybe wanna go home, doc?” Elize suggested, her voice quiet and gentle. “I’m loving the vibes we’ve got going on up here, but if someone walked through that door and interrupted me right now, I might just attack them. I can’t imagine that’d be good for the reputation I’m trying to cultivate here.” She grinned in that sweet, lopsided way she did when she was joking around, and Vervain wanted to kiss her again right then and there.

… Well, why shouldn’t she?

She leaned forward. This kiss was shorter, a brief little brush of facsimile lips on Elize’s cheek. Still, the act filled Elize’s face with her own form of blushblooms, pink spreading outward in a beautiful little portrait of human anatomy.

“Yes, please,” Vervain whispered.

Tilly was having an excellent day! It looked like it might rain, and Camellia’s vines were all over its body, and Tilly really liked both vines and rain. Tilly rocked and nuzzled up against a bloom that drifted close to its face, content to breathe in the smell of its Maestrix and luxuriate in the first drops of water that fell from the synthetic sky.

“Interesting…” Camellia remarked, and Tilly’s dazed eyes drifted toward the most beautiful plant in the entire history of ever.

“Yes, Maestrix?”

“Oh; just a curious little message I received from Naamah. Evidently, she bumped into our odd little rescue up in one of the balcony lounges.” Camellia gestured to the various little recesses in the walls far, far above. Tilly preferred being curled up into a little crystalbug nest in its Maestrix’s vines in the park, so they didn’t really go up there a whole lot! But it knew where they were, at least!

“Mhm??”

“She didn’t say much about the interaction, but it sounds like Vervain is enjoying herself. Good for her.” Tilly could feel a little pang of sadness from the conductor of its heart, which just absolutely wouldn’t do. It bumped its head aggressively against the flower again, then nibbled on the side a bit.

Its Maestrix made a sound equivalent to a gasp, a vine curling about Tilly’s neck. “You little flirt,” Camellia murmured. Tilly nodded! It was a little flirt.

“Fine. You brought this upon yourself, my sweet instrument.”

Oh, yes. Tilly absolutely brought this on itself.

Chapter 15: In Which Elize Finds a Home

Chapter Notes

CW: this chapter talks about some sexual trauma.

Elize learned a valuable lesson today. No, not about kissing—well, that was a very valuable lesson, but—in this case, it was that she was a genius. ‘And humble, too,’ Poppy chimed in.

‘Thanks!’

Yeah, she could feel Vervain thirsting over her. She couldn’t get the specifics, that wasn’t how the rhythms worked, but she could tell something about her behavior had set Vervain’s desire spinning toward her gravity. That would have made their walk home really complicated, given how her little friend (the one in her trousers, not the Poppy in her brain) liked to emote.

But Elize was a genius, and she remembered that Poppy had managed to prevent muscle movement in her arm earlier. ‘Could you—’

‘Are you really going to ask me to do this.’

‘Well, unless you wanna walk home with the stiffy. Alternatively, we could shut off Vervain’s brain again, but that might make the problem worse before it makes it better.’

Elize could feel Poppy savoring that idea, and admittedly she was too, but that was not helping. ‘Fine! Fine, just… Try not to move for a moment, figuring out the structure of our brain is weird.’

‘You’re the best, babe.’

She could feel something swirling around in her skull, which was weird and kinda hot, and she bet if she subjected Vervain to a similar sensation, the slutty little plant would be an absolute mess over it. Poppy brushed against something behind her right eye that made her taste the color fuschia.

Wow! Wow, okay, that was weird. Yup, no more trouser problem. ‘You break it, you buy it,’ she warned Poppy, who pulsated with something resembling a laugh. Elize pressed her awareness into the section of the brain Poppy had messed with, and was baffled to feel exactly where the changes had happened. Knowing what your own mind feels like is a surreal experience that Elize was slowly getting used to.

She could walk home like this at least! And she could hold Vervain’s hand the whole time, which was exactly what she should be doing.

‘Home.’ Elize chewed on that as they walked.

She hadn’t had one of those in a while, and yet she’d already started thinking of the bedroom she had with Vervain as home. It was far more spacious than she’d had anywhere in the last decade. Roots, it was more comfortable than her childhood bedroom had been: back when she was still pretending that her father was a good and decent man who only sold weapons to good and decent men and that she, also, would one day grow up to be a good and decent man.

She wondered what Vervain might say about that.

Goodness, she wondered what Vervain would say about a lot of things.

Elize’s eyes trailed over to the affini whose hand she’d claimed properly for her own (at least, until they got home). Vervain had finally stopped broadcasting the flustered energy that she’d had when Elize had been talking about salacious things, instead settling into her normal anxious humming from being out in a public space.

She also still hadn’t made herself any bigger than Elize had ordered.

Elize tried not to let her mind wander on that, it’d just get her way too excited again and they still had a few minutes of walking to reach the hab.

Instead, she squeezed Vervain’s hand a little tighter and felt that anxiety squish between her fingertips and dissipate, just a little, into nothingness. A giddy sort of excitement took its place, one that Elize couldn’t help but feel a pang of longing over.

‘You’ve got it bad,’ Poppy teased.

‘Pot calling the kettle hot,’ Elize snapped back.

Poppy scrambled a bit over that. After all, Elize could feel that she wasn’t the only one in her system who had a crush.

Elize wanted to talk to said crush.

Well, no. What she actually wanted to do was push Vervain into one of the alley areas they’d passed by, hold her up against the wall, and kiss her again. But. That probably wouldn’t help with Vervain’s anxiety about being seen in public, and neither would talking, so they just kinda kept walking in awkward silence that was absolutely eating her up inside.

“Are you all right?” Vervain poked.

“Oh; yeah, I just figured you were… I dunno, maybe a little uncomfortable with the idea of talking about what just happened while we’re—” Elize cut off as they turned a corner with another couple affini and pets nearby.

They’d seated around a picnic table, laughing and resonating happiness and rubbing the belly of a creature that seemed primarily composed of horns and teeth. Elize had no idea what it was, but it kept them distracted enough to not notice her and Vervain slip by.

She could feel the way Vervain relaxed as they turned another bend out of sight.

“… That’s probably wise,” Vervain murmured, before Elize could finish her sentence.

“Is it, though?” Elize pushed back. “I mean, we just met the captain of the ship who made it quite clear that she was less concerned about my existence than she was about our lack of communication. If she’s a basis to go off of, do we really have all that much to worry about?”

“I’m not certain I would consider Naamah to be a perfect representation of the whole of the Compact. She’s a little… peculiar, even by our standards.”

“Vervain, be honest with me: Are you genuinely worried about them asking me questions? Or are you worried that they’ll ask you questions?” Elize could feel the way that her affini tensed up, and she tried to comfort her by squeezing that hand a little tighter. Vervain withdrew hers instead. Dirt.

“I don’t know what you’re talking about. Of course I’m worried about you, you’re… we’re in new territory, Elize. And you could… I’m worried about talking about this in public, what if someone hears me?” Vervain glanced around nervously, like someone was waiting to jump her.

‘You just had to say it,’ Poppy sighed.

‘What? Oh—oh, rot.’

Outside of Vervain’s hab stood a familiar mass of yellow flowers and redwood. Astrae Zonale, First Bloom, seemed to rustle with discontent. Vervain’s rustling was quite different, as the coiled spring in her seemed to grow tight and she snapped into a higher register than she usually used. “Mx. Zonale! To what do I owe the pleasure?”

“Good afternoon, doctor! Terribly sorry to intrude; I was just curious how things were going!” She swirled forward; the way she moved reminded Elize of the way wind would kick up sand on her third floret’s homeworld.

Uh.

‘Sorry, that was mine.’

Elize would worry about that later.

“Quite well! I’m afraid we may not be very good company at the moment, however; we were just planning to retire for the day. Is there something you needed?” Elize felt Vervain’s signature fade into the buzzing, uncomfortable vortex it had been earlier in the week. She must have started the jammies—

‘Jammerblooms.’

‘Whatever.’

—jammies back up. Elize would talk to her about that later.

Right. Panic attack.
