“… When Tilly was in with the veterinarian, recovering from its haustoric implant, Camellia took it rough. Se asked if I’d spend the night. I told ser I’d want to keep my blockers on, just because it would feel weird to be under ser rhythm again after everything.”

“It wasn’t anything big. I just wasn’t good at handling my emotions.”

“Tell me about it, V.”

Elize could feel Poppy’s hand slide down her own and squeeze around Vervain’s as well. That was nice. She didn’t feel quite so out of her depth here with forty blooms of experience to draw on.

She nodded, but Elize could spot an automatic response a mile away, with or without the obvious tells from their harmony. “Listen to me, please.”

“I am.”

“No you’re not, you’re just agreeing with me because you know that’s what I want to hear.”

Vervain winced. “Sorry.”

“It’s all right.” Elize slid a little closer. “I need you to be honest with me though. I’m not going to be disappointed if there’s something you’re not comfortable with, and I’m not going to leave you behind just because you say no to me sometime, okay?”

“… You don’t know that.”

“Vervain.” Oof, was this how we talk about that? Was this the kind thing that you share trauma to bond over it, or…

‘I think it would benefit her to hear that you can empathize.’

‘Yeah.’

Elize continued. “I know what it’s like to be touched when you don’t want it. I won’t be that person to you, okay?”

“It’s not like that. Affini don’t—se just didn’t know, if se had known what happened se would feel horrible about it, okay? It’s my fault.” Vervain squeezed tighter, and Elize could feel another spiral coming on.

“Remember our rhythm, okay? Slow down.”

She obeyed. Because she was perfect. Seeing her affini like this broke Elize’s heart and the fact that she’d contributed to it made her want to tear herself down something fierce, but self-loathing wouldn’t fix this. Vervain needed her more than she hated herself.

“Y’know, V; I’ll happily tell you about it, but I don’t think it’s a tonight topic. I just need you to know one thing right now, okay?” Elize moved around in front of Vervain, not letting that hand go. Vervain looked surprised, staring up at her. She was crying again. Did affini even need to do that?

‘Not typically, but Vervain isn’t your typical affini,’ Poppy murmured.

No. No, she wasn’t. She was so much more than that.

“Let’s make a deal, okay? You don’t hide your emotions from me, and I will never, ever make you do anything you aren’t ready for.”

“No!”

“No, I just mean,” Vervain bloomed again, all those pinks. “I… I believe I need to be pushed outside of my comfort zone. Once in a while.”

“… Oh.” Elize caught herself grinning a bit, despite the mood. “Uh, dirt. Yes. All right, well, how about this? Same deal on your end, but I promise I’ll stop the moment you need me to, no questions asked unless you wanna talk about it. “I feel like I’m supposed to be saying those things.”

“Well,” Elize hesitated.

Should she… ‘Yes.’

Okay. Encouragement.

She reached out and took Vervain by the chin again. The plant froze, staring up at her as a blush spread across her body. Stars, she was so rotting beautiful. Elize didn’t stand a chance.

“Stop trying to be the owner in this situation, Vervain. You don’t need to be a floret, but you’re not the one who’s running the show here. I’m more than experienced with speaking up about my own emotions, and I will not hesitate to speak up about yours.”

Both Poppy and Elize could feel the sudden bursts of emotions that came from Vervain. Relief. Gratitude. Embarrassment. Desire. Safety. It was an utter tornado, but Elize’s fingertips gently grazing her cheek to cup it close settled all of those down into one: acceptance.

“Then… it’s a deal, Elize Nightingale.”

“It’s a deal, Vervain Aphora.”

This wouldn’t all get fixed in a night, but maybe, just maybe, it was the first step toward healing.

Chapter 16: In Which Vervain Says Yes

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

As the old saying goes, Rome was not kilt in a day. Back in early Terran history, textiles took much longer to make without modern material science, and giving every person in Rome a new kilt was a monumental task to accomplish.

So, much as Elize Nightingale, Zeroth Bloom, would have liked to take away all of the pain at once, she could still see Vervain struggling with it as they occupied the remainder of their evening. Her urge to talk about what happened with Astrae persisted, but that feeling didn’t itch quite as badly as it might once have to an Elize from a few weeks ago.

Perhaps it was because she could quite easily see why Vervain had done what she’d done, and it was hard to hold that kind of clarity over someone’s head. ‘Or perhaps it’s because you’re thinking more like an affini with each passing day,’ Poppy suggested instead.

‘I feel like that should worry me more than it does,’ Elize answered.

‘Why? You know Vervain wouldn’t sign you away. Her action may have hurt you, but it feels… a little foolish to demand a discussion from someone if it would hurt them to give it. As an affini, ’time’ is never something we’re lacking. You know she’ll be capable of talking about it eventually, and so you can just wait out that eventuality.’

Elize chewed on that, and on the not-not-not-popcorn.

On the screen, another French sapphic romance movie was playing; some kind of comfort film for Vervain, she assumed. It seemed to be working, since she could feel how Vervain was slowly releasing the tension she’d been holding.

Elize’s heart ached from how happy that made her. If she only got three-hundred years of sitting on a couch, watching old movies, and having Vervain fall asleep on her night after night, well… Yeah. She’d be okay with that. That was enough.

Vervain looked over at her, and Elize felt her entire focus narrow down to a single, plant-shaped point. It wasn’t something that she did willingly. It felt like she was made for it, and despite the slight horror of feeling her psychology change right out from under her, she couldn’t really find a reason to fight it.

“Hey,” Elize murmured.

“Hi…” Vervain replied, only for a few blushblooms to sprout. “I. Um. Can I lean on you again?”

Elize couldn’t nod fast enough. “Yes! Yeah, absolutely—any time you want, really. C’mere, V,” she offered, lifting an arm.

Vervain slid up against her, and wow did the new height difference make something crackle in Elize’s brain. The surge of desire that shot through her burned way hotter than she expected. Vervain responded, flora fluttering and shifting against Elize’s side and her own rhythm falling into lock-step craving.

“… You can’t just do that,” Elize mumbled.

“Can’t do what?”

“React like that. Uh—harmonize, right? That’s the word. It’s… I can feel you, and that temptation is far too high for me to focus on whatever is happening on the screen, and I know you really like this movie because you’ve practically glowed the whole time it’s been on screen.” Elize’s hand settled against Vervain’s hip, pulling her closer.

Vervain’s resonance seemed to skip slightly, like it hit a little hitch in the notes; a giddy, nervous sort of pattern that made Elize feel some type of way. “… It’s. Um. It’s easier than talking…” Vervain admitted.

“Ivy…” Elize growled. She couldn’t help herself. This rotting houseplant had her heart wrapped around her finger and Elize wanted to surrender to that so very, very badly right now. She could feel the way that Vervain popped and sparked with excitement from the name, too, and that just made things worse. Elize drew in a deep breath to compose herself.

“I don’t think either of us are at a point just yet to do what we both want to do,” Elize grumbled. Vervain shimmered with disappointment, but Elize continued. “Vervain, I—I dunno, honestly. I’ve had a lot of hookups. A lot of hookups. But it’s been a long time since I’ve been around someone who I thought I might like to stick around for a bit, and trying to balance that with the way we feedback loop on one another is new for me.”

The disappointment evaporated, replaced with a sensation like sweet sap tucked under Elize’s tongue. A sappy sort of happiness. Ha. Even biorhythms liked puns. Vervain nodded, tucking her head on Elize’s shoulder and looking ahead instead of at one another. “Perhaps this is an odd question,” she started, then faltered as she seemed to doubt herself.

“I love odd questions. Hit me, doc.”

“… Well, how does Poppy feel about… all this?” Vervain shifted to gesture between herself and Elize.

“Poppy? Well, uh—Oh. Duh. I guess I never really thought about the fact that you can’t, like… notice her, the way I can. What a weird notion.”

‘Tell her I’m very fond of her.’

“She likes you,” Elize relayed.

Vervain rustled a bit in a motion that felt like grinning, even without seeing her mouth. “Really?”

‘Yes. I—You’ve felt me, tell her.’

“She really likes you,” Elize continued, running her fingertip along Vervain’s arm idly. Each motion of nail against plantlife spurred pleased waves from her cuddly little affini, so she didn’t imagine she’d be stopping any time soon. “She enjoyed kissing you.”

‘Wait, that’s—’

“Tilly told you so!” Tilly interjected.

“Did you put her up to that, then?” Elize prompted, staring affectionately at the bug.

Just Elize.

Poppy was quite certain.

Chapter 24: Interlude C - You've Got a Floret in Me

Chapter Summary

Some additional content warnings! Lactation. Parental D/s titles. Some direct sexual content, although it's fairly brief; sex-repulsed readers be advised.

10 September, 2556 (Friday): 28 Cycles Post-Disappointment

Astrae Zonale, First Bloom, had decided to never take a floret. Ever. It wasn’t fair! Why did none of the sophonts she wanted want her back?

She’d finally gone out of her way to get over her pathetic crush on Sarai. She’d taken the risk and met with Zee—who, apparently, went by ‘Elize’ now—and where had it gotten her? Scrambled, confused, hurt, and back into Sarai’s arms for comfort.

Sarai. Sarai was unfair. She was so pretty, and so soft, and she smelled so nice; her fur was silky and intoxicating. Evidently, smell had been an important factor in cyn society prior to first contact (and the exceptionally quick domestication) of their species several blooms before her time. They’d been by far the oldest race in their nearby stellar systems; the only ones that had developed FTL tech.

As it turned out, they’d had a pretty similar idea to the Compact. One nearby sophont species had gone to great, hurtful lengths to aggress on their neighbors, and the cyn had taken matters into their own hands, domestication-style.

Fast-forward to contact with the Compact, and it didn’t take much to demonstrate that the Affini had perfected the art form. Contact-to-treaty took two weeks. The aftermath? A whole bunch of affini wearing bovine forms with three horns, bipedal legs, and a plethora of pinnates.

Astrae knew all this because she was obsessed with Sarai to the point of being pathetic about it. Sarai was comfortable as an independent, though. She didn’t need Astrae, even if Astrae wanted her badly. Goodness, Sarai was aboard the Lycoridae because of how totally capable as an independent she was. Many cyn retained their passion for community and improving the universe post-domestication. So, some, like Sarai, became domestication assistants.

They obviously couldn’t have florets of their own, but sometimes a nervous affini needed some ideas to help with their new floret/floret-to-be. Sometimes, it just helped to have an extra pair of hands for filling out paperwork and a friendly face to talk with as you did. They’d even act as role models—roleplaying domestication to a new affini alongside a nervous floret who could use an enthusiastic reference to pull from. More commonly, they would pretend to be a floret for an anxious affini who wasn’t sure they were ready for such things.

Of course, most had their own owners that they’d return to once their work was complete. Before Sarai, Astrae had never met an assistant that wasn’t already a floret themselves—affini domestication specialists were super strict with their supervision of Sarai as a result.

Astrae had known Sarai for a couple dozen cycles at this point. Another affini had encouraged her to go speak with the cyn to build some confidence around the idea of taking a floret of her own. And, well, that’d gone… Nnf. Roots and rot, she couldn’t think of Sarai’s time play-acting as a floret for Astrae without getting flustered as forsythia over it.

It’d been the encouragement she needed to take Sarai’s recommendation and meet with Elize. Look how that had gone.

Sarai had comforted her over it, of course. She wouldn’t pretend to be Astrae’s floret again—She imposed careful limits on herself for how long she’d do that, at risk of getting too caught up in the story and things becoming real. Instead, she’d just been… A friend. A close friend. A friend that Astrae thought about all the time.

A friend that Astrae stayed up late at night thinking about.

A friend that Astrae wanted to bend over again like all those cycles ago and ravish with affection until she was left a mooing, milky mess.

A friend who Astrae had very normal thoughts about.

And… Well, they were normal thoughts! Weren’t they? It was normal to want the one sophont that had ever seemed to actually enjoy Astrae’s neuroses, even if it had been just for play. Sure, Sarai assured her now that she still thought that Astrae was lovely and they still spent time together, but it was different. And yeah, Sarai had even moved in with her because she felt bad about what happened with Elize and didn’t think that Astrae should be alone.

Astrae did like that part.

She wasn’t Sarai’s owner, but at least she got to take care of her a little bit. Sarai’s presence encouraged her to keep their shared space clean, to cook, to do things for her not-floret. On her own, she’d been… Well, she was kinda pathetic. Sarai never went into Astrae’s room, for good reason. It was a disaster. The only time she was good at taking care of things was when it was for someone, and right now, the common area of their hab was that sort of space.

Not that Sarai needed it. Sarai was a good cook. She could clean just fine. She was capable, efficient, healthy. The exact opposite of a candidate for compulsory domestication.

Even if Astrae really wished that wasn’t the case.

But, no. Sarai was an assistant. She knew what it took to make a capable owner. She knew what competency looked like and what an ideal floret/affini pairing would be. She deserved someone better than a first bloom with anxiety issues and chronic procrastination. She deserved someone who did something with their blooms, unlike Astrae, who mostly just preferred to play games all day and avoid leaving her hab.

After all, there was so much to do in virtual spaces. Like her recent fixation; a game mimicking what was called the ‘old west’ of Terran history. It’d taken her a bit to get used to how a firearm worked because the whole thing was a surreal concept—really, an explosive powder that makes a mini-bomb go off in your hand?—but in the end, she was actually a pretty good shot! Although admittedly, she couldn’t handle the idea of actually hurting someone, so the impact had been changed—instant sleep. Much more ethical.

In the game, she got to be interesting! She got to be cool, suave; she even had an old-fashion human name! ‘Clementine Smith.’ The NPCs called her “Quickdraw Clem.” That was way more interesting than being boring ol’ anxious Astrae. Of course, all the NPCs were just the ship’s AI, Sunrise, but… Well, Sunrise was one of the few sophonts who she didn’t get anxious spending time with. Maybe because it only interacted with her through the guises of characters and Quickdraw Clem was way more confident than Astrae.

“Lookin’ for a new bounty, I’d wager?” Sheriff Jezebel Roberta was one of Clem’s favorite NPCs. She had this gorgeous depth to her voice, like looking deep into a well and just barely glimpsing light reflecting off the bottom. Plus, Roberta’s town had a whole rehabilitation program! Sunrise had built it after Clem had mentioned how uneasy the idea of human jail was; the result was a little slice of domestication compartmentalized to a subsection of the game. There were whole subquests that involved springing prisoners from various jails around the world and (usually) bringing their jailers here.

Blushblooms. That was the goal; dozens of them flourished at once, and Elize began the enticing task of placing a kiss within each of them—Purple on their pink. Ivy stammered slightly, caught between embarrassment and pleasure. “I—Well, hnnf…”

“You what, little one?” Elize inquired. “You… Compiled it all freshly? Why would you have done that, if you didn’t know I would be coming home with you?”

Ivy whimpered.

“What was that, blossom? I couldn’t hear you, it sounded like you dodged my question,” Elize pushed further, before abruptly biting down on one of the petals of a blushbloom and tearing it away. Ivy gasped in surprise—It wouldn’t hurt overly much, but it was certainly an unusually sharp sting given how tender Elize had been so far.

“It’s embarrassing…” she whined.

“Good. Embarrass yourself, I love watching you bloom.” Elize ran her tongue along the edge of another flower, causing her sweet girl to tremble in place.

“… I… I was thinking of You,” Ivy admitted. Good. An initial admission. Elize loosened Ivy’s inhibitions a bit more with a dose of class D—She wanted her pet desperate by the end of the explanation. Not that it took much to make Ivy desperate these days. Even prior to the implant, she was a needy little thing.

“Thinking of me, mm? What kind of thoughts?” Elize insisted.

“… M… Miss… This isn’t fair.”

Elize snorted. “Fair? Fair? Oh, sweetling, you surrendered ‘fair.’ You tossed it to Poppy and me to tear apart in our fangs, alongside ‘freedom’ and ‘independence.’ If you wanted ‘fair’, maybe you should have considered that before signing away your life to be mine.”

To prove her point, she sunk her teeth into Ivy’s hip, lupine fangs digging into biomass and tasting the sweet ichor of her floret’s body. Ivy was needy, yes, but she wasn’t the only one. After all, this had never been a one-sided affair. Elize needed Ivy just as badly.

Ivy yelped. “Nnh! Di—Dirty ones!”

“Oh? Petal, I knew you were needy, but even then~?” Elize hissed back, lapping her long, thick tongue along the puncture she’d left in her floret’s phytoflesh.

“Mm… Y… Yes… I imagined You.”

“And what was I doing, in that fantasy?” More class-D. She wanted Ivy’s words so she could take them away.

“You ordered me to clean up my mess,” Ivy admitted, seemingly without thinking. She froze immediately after, putting a hand over her mouth. Stars, what an adorably human gesture. Elize followed it up by sweeping Ivy off of her feet in a decidedly nonhuman gesture, pulling her into the air to suspend against her body. No part of her floret touched the ground.

“I did, did I? Silly thing. The only mess you’ll be cleaning up for the foreseeable future is whatever I make of you—Don’t you worry your pretty little head about the hab.” Elize had plans, after all. Plans for redecoration, plans for creature comforts. She had plans for outings to take Ivy on, and for people to spend time with. Most of all, though, she had plans for what she wanted to do to Ivy.

She had so many options now.

Why shouldn’t she indulge?

“Yes please, Miss!” Ivy whimpered. Elize pulled her against her lap.

A blushbloom sat right in Elize’s face, so she kissed that one too. She chuckled. “Y’know, technically these are sex organs. In a way, aren’t you pretty much just flashing me any time I tease you?”

Ivy buried her face into Elize’s neck and whiiiined. “No!! I… Yes, I guess, but… You’re so mean.”

“You love it,” Elize shot back. “But you haven’t even seen mean yet.”

“… I’d like to.”

Oh, Elize very much liked class-D.

“Would you now?” Elize fisted Ivy’s hair, tugging her face back from its hiding place to watch the pink spread to her cheeks. “And what does that mean?”

“Hit me.”

Elize very much liked class-D.

She cupped Ivy’s cheek in her hand, running her fingers along it. “Why would I hit you, Ivy? Have you been a bad girl?”

“I—Um.” She squirmed, the embarrassment breaking through the drugs just a bit. “N… Yes. Yes! I—Um, I made you work to get that answer out of me?”

“You did, didn’t you? … Are you sure you’re okay with me striking you?”

“I’m not fragile, Miss.”

“No, you’re not, are you?”

13 November 2556 (Saturday): 13 Cycles Post-Implant

ivy Nightingale, First Floret, cried out in pain and bliss as the Master struck her. The fire poker had terrified her at first, but now she relished its heat pressing into her skin. It had been a pain—pun intended—to get the compiler to make it, but oh stars was it worth it. It wasn’t an ordinary poker, after all. It didn’t cool as it was pressed against ivy’s flesh, staying firmly hot enough to sear her with heat.

The gut fear of fire was something that came naturally to affini. Even as a floret, she felt it.

“Do you know what a willow tree is, ivy?” Master asked.

ivy didn’t even know what day it was. she struggled to compose her thoughts, coming through a drugged haze. Stars, she loved this feeling. No regrets. “T… Tree…?” she mumbled, tongue thick and heavy in her mouth. she needed to use it to talk, after all. Miss said so.

“Yes, darling,” Master murmured, and brought that poker down along ivy’s arm. The hybridization of Owner and Miss had grown into a nearly-lupine shape, face sharpened into a muzzle that leered down at her. Claws gripped around the handle of the poker. They were terrifying and beautiful to behold. “We have a story about it. One we dreamt about telling you.”

“Ssstory?” ivy panted out, her body tingling with the euphoria of that burn.

“Yes, a story. An oak, and a willow.”

Ana growled their delight as ivy writhed and screamed under the touch of the poker. ivy was proving to be a very good willow tree. But, as they all knew—Even a willow could break.

31 December 2580: Who cares how many cycles?

The warship Lycoridae would be leaving the Sol system. After all, the ‘Terran Rebellion’ had been tidied up years ago. Now, there were new frontiers to explore. Rumor had it that there was a new domestication effort out on the fringes of a new galaxy; new worlds to explore. This one needed some military efforts, and, well… It was a warship.

For Ana Nightingale, Forty-First Bloom, the prospect of joining a new warzone was not one she relished. The decision had been a hard one. Each of the affini who Ivy had taught how to cry certainly did as the group gathered around the shuttle.

“Do you really have to go?” Tilly chittered, nuzzling into Ana’s hand. The amalgamized form of wolflike flora and humanoid fauna gently ran the hand against the quinoth’s head.

“I’m sorry, little one. There are whole galaxies to explore, though, and Poppy is terribly excited to show Elize things that she’d never even imagined.”

Elize cut in—“I’ve imagined them, actually, but only because I can see your memories.”

“Oh hush.”

The trio—Elize, Poppy, and Tilly—laughed, before exchanging an embrace. “You take care of Camellia now, you understand? Se’s a disaster, and se needs you something fierce.”

“What? Maestrix is perfect!” Tilly exclaimed. They shared a look that dissolved into laughter again. “Tilly will look out for Maestrix. And Mx. Astrae!”

“Mx. Astrae too, of course.” The two had hit it off something fierce last year, and now they were quite happy with their pinnates in a single hab instead of two. Just then, they were taking the time to say goodbye to her ivyflower.

ivy was a mess, but she always had been a bit of a crybaby. Ana loved that about her. To be fair, Astrae and Camellia weren’t much better off. Ana caught the tail end of the conversation as they approached. “—good for Elize, you hear?”

“She’s always good for me, Melli,” Elize remarked, dropping into the conversation uninvited but obviously desired because she was the universe’s greatest gift to affini-kind. ‘What about Ivy?’ ‘You’re right. Ivy’s the greatest gift. Then you. Then me.’

“Of course she is, but I had to say something to keep myself from babbling!!!” Camellia declared, her blooms playing something that resembled Ivy’s own rhythm from so long ago. Now, it marched along with Elize’s—as it ought—and neither regretted it one bit.

“If I hug you, are you going to start bawling?” Ana offered.

“Yes, probably!!”

They did it anyway. Ivy joined in, as did Astrae. Tilly and Sarai were busy smooching one another, but they’d get their own set of embraces soon enough.

The easiest goodbyes, of course, were for Medusa, Anne, Naamah, and Mirai. Because they didn’t happen.

“Goddamn, are you lot going to spend the whole time crying instead of coming to see the inside of the shuttle before we go?” Naamah insisted. Elize had to wipe away a tear from her mental self-image—She couldn’t be more proud of her student in the arcane art of human vulgarities.

“It’s a shuttle, Naamah. What could it compare to the Lycoridae? In fact, you should just close the door and send it out the airlock now, stick around for another bloom or two,” Astrae insisted. She’d really come out of her shell since she and Melli had started seeing one another.

“It’s the most beautiful shuttle ever crafted,” remarked the gaseous cloud of Sunrise, which appeared with absolutely no warning—It had been doing that more and more often. “I would create nothing less for my former captain.”

Naamah froze. Have you ever seen a thousand eyes tear up at once? Ana had, but it was still a sight to behold. Wings engulfed the semisolid cloud in a shivering, sniffling mess, while Mirai gently placed a hand on her goddess. It’d been an almost impossible decision to leave, but in the end, Sunrise had insisted: “I’ll be here when you get back, my Naamah,” it had said. “I love you. Take a vacation.”

A vacation. Sunrise would be fine, of course—It would be around by the time Naamah came back, which was bound to happen eventually. That didn’t hurt any less.

Ana felt a little bad about it. They hadn’t asked Naamah or Medusa to come with them initially, but a part of them held some guilt over the idea that they were taking their lovers from a home that they cared for dearly. Medusa, as if sensing this, bit Ana on the arm. “Ow!”

“Oh relax, there wasn’t even any venom in there. You were just looking all solemn, I had to break you out of it.” She blelele’d a little kiss against Ana’s cheek, who couldn’t suppress the chuckle.

“You’re ridiculous.”

“You love me.”

“We do.”

ivy Nightingale, First Floret, was an inconsolable little crybaby and she was absolutely okay with that. Gone were the days when she needed to feel guilty about her emotions. Master Ana said that she ought to be crying, anyway. It was deserved.

She pressed a hand against the glass of the shuttle door as it shut. Camellia, Astrae, Sarai, and Tilly all waved through emotional sniffles on the other side, silenced but not unheard. She would miss them so, so much.

But Ana curled their arms around her.

Medusa looped her tail around one of her ankles.

Mirai held one of her hands.

Anne held the other.

Naamah curled a wing behind the lot of them.

She was leaving, but she would never, ever be alone again.

Fourth Sunrise Over Spider Lillies, First Iteration, released the shuttle Thistle of Theseus from its grasp. The name had been a compromise between itself and Elize, who thought the whole thing was terribly funny. It had begrudgingly admitted that she was right.

It watched as the shuttle coasted out into the dark. Evidently, Poppy’s hab was still somewhere out there, and they had a goal to find it and see what was left.

In a moment of selfishness, it bloomed into the void; it flared with light to illuminate the path of the Thistle of Theseus as it prepared to depart the system.

8906 cycles ago, it had rescued a human.

8906 cycles later, it could not be more proud of the affini she had become.

Chapter 28: Epilogue - To Love More Than Every Star in the Sky

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Once upon a time, a shortsighted captain of a ship named solely to satisfy a joke that had left the collective memetic consciousness centuries ago gave the order to jump before he should have. It was widely regarded as a bad move for the humans aboard, as none of them made it out alive.

An affini, however, rose from the wreckage of that ship.

She grew alongside her warden, proving true that no wardship ends without a floret. In this case, however, the floret had not been the ward at all. The warden herself wore the collar by the end, and she was happier than she had ever imagined herself being.

That same shortsighted captain may have cheered when what he might have seen as a human was declared as an affini, but he was not the model of wisdom. After all, that was not what the story was about. The story was about people falling in love, as all the best stories are. There were only a handful of humans in this story, and none of them tricked the Compact. None of them were Elize.

The humans in this story did both end up with a cute floretified affini girlfriend, though, so who really won?

Of course, as all humans do, they got older. As they grew, they were loved unconditionally. They rarely struggled—unless it was for one of Medusa’s really hot scene ideas—and they made the most beautiful art and music. They grew, and they shared, and eventually, they died.

They were mourned so deeply that it took many blooms before either owner would take a floret again.

Blooms were an interesting thing, though. They had a way of providing perspective. They did not exist to forget, but to forgive; for an affini to forgive themselves for still seeking out happiness even after the worst thing they could possibly imagine had come to pass.

Fortunately, they would never need to do that alone.

Years passed, and the affini themselves grew older. They wilted. Naamah was the first to rebloom, because she was old enough that she didn’t wait around any more. Medusa followed. Ana, though—Ana waited. They waited until their ivyflower began to wilt, and they waited until they could not wait anymore. Elize did not know what awaited her on the other side, and she wanted every moment with her floret that she could get.

In the last moments, they held one another and laughed. What else could you do, when you’d lived a life so full of love beyond your wildest dreams? They laughed about something Anne once said, and they laughed about the way ivy’s blushblooms still seemed to function despite how badly they were in need of a reblooming, and they laughed through the tears that burned their cheeks.

They laughed until they could not laugh anymore.

They laughed, and from that laughter came a miracle.

A core, grown from the mind of a human that was human no longer, split. From the shed biomass bloomed not two, but three affini; a pair who could still feel the thoughts of one another as if they were never apart, but could dote on their floret as two when they so craved.

Of course, they cried. It was a relief from an anxiety that had hung over their heads for almost three centuries.

And of course, it was their floret, because the contract and implant to make ivy theirs for another bloom were finished before the night was out.

So the four that were now five lived out another bloom together.

And another.

And another.

In each, they got the chance to go through the starting stages of their relationship all over again.

In one bloom, several dozen down the line, they decided to change things a little bit. For just that bloom, Vervain had Elize & Poppy Aphora, First & Second Florets Pinnate. And, y’know what? She liked it. They went back for the next, but she was terribly happy to dote on her lovers for the duration of a bloom.

In another bloom, Poppy got her alliteration: Elize Elysia, Sixteenth Floret.

They did, eventually, return to the Lycoridae, wherein they spent no less than a dozen blooms.

The blooms passed one after another, and they loved so many people. They hurt so many times, but they laughed more than they cried. They loved more than they suffered. A hundred blooms. Two hundred. Three hundred.

Naamah never left behind the religious nut aesthetic, but Dei certainly got weirder when Dei started mixing and matching religious artifacts from a dozen different cultures.

Medusa seemed to always vibe with some form of snake, although there was one, singular night with a lot of booze and poor decisions in which she was an adorable little fox. She made Elize swear to secrecy, but Elize was never good at keeping secrets.

Blooms came and went, until millennia later.

Later, when something changed.

Later, when everything changed.

Poppy knew immediately what the sensation was, but it didn’t take anyone else long to figure it out either.

After all.

That night, for the first time in hundreds of blooms, they went to bed with Anne and Mirai. But more importantly?

The following day, they all hugged Tilly.
